=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two integers, n and threshold, as well as a directed weighted graph of n nodes numbered from 0 to n - 1. The graph is represented by a 2D integer array edges, where edges[i] = [A_i, B_i, W_i] indicates that there is an edge going from node A_i to node B_i with weight W_i.
You have to remove some edges from this graph (possibly none), so that it satisfies the following conditions:

Node 0 must be reachable from all other nodes.
The maximum edge weight in the resulting graph is minimized.
Each node has at most threshold outgoing edges.

Return the minimum possible value of the maximum edge weight after removing the necessary edges. If it is impossible for all conditions to be satisfied, return -1.
 
Example 1:

Input: n = 5, edges = [[1,0,1],[2,0,2],[3,0,1],[4,3,1],[2,1,1]], threshold = 2
Output: 1
Explanation:

Remove the edge 2 -> 0. The maximum weight among the remaining edges is 1.

Example 2:

Input: n = 5, edges = [[0,1,1],[0,2,2],[0,3,1],[0,4,1],[1,2,1],[1,4,1]], threshold = 1
Output: -1
Explanation: 
It is impossible to reach node 0 from node 2.

Example 3:

Input: n = 5, edges = [[1,2,1],[1,3,3],[1,4,5],[2,3,2],[3,4,2],[4,0,1]], threshold = 1
Output: 2
Explanation: 

Remove the edges 1 -> 3 and 1 -> 4. The maximum weight among the remaining edges is 2.

Example 4:

Input: n = 5, edges = [[1,2,1],[1,3,3],[1,4,5],[2,3,2],[4,0,1]], threshold = 1
Output: -1

 
Constraints:

2 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= threshold <= n - 1
1 <= edges.length <= min(10^5, n * (n - 1) / 2).
edges[i].length == 3
0 <= A_i, B_i < n
A_i != B_i
1 <= W_i <= 10^6
There may be multiple edges between a pair of nodes, but they must have unique weights.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minMaxWeight(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]], threshold: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

class Solution:
    def minMaxWeight(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]], threshold: int) -> int:
        rev = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        max_w = 0
        for a,b,w in edges:
            rev[b].append((a,w))
            if w > max_w: max_w = w
        # maybe if threshold == 0? Not needed but could handle? If threshold==0, only node0 can have 0 outgoing? For other nodes no outgoing, impossible unless n=1 but n>=2. But constraints threshold>=1. Ignore or handle? Could include if threshold <=0 return -1? But threshold type int maybe not. Not necessary.
        from collections import deque
        def ok(limit):
            seen = [False]*n
            seen[0] = True
            q = deque([0])
            cnt = 1
            while q:
                u = q.popleft()
                for v,w in rev[u]:
                    if w <= limit and not seen[v]:
                        seen[v] = True
                        cnt += 1
                        q.append(v)
            return cnt == n
        if not ok(max_w): return -1
        lo, hi = 1, max_w
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo+hi)//2
            if ok(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid+1
        return lo

Now, let's decide final code. We can include:

```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minMaxWeight(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]], threshold: int) -> int:
        rev = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        max_w = 0
        for a, b, w in edges:
            rev[b].append((a, w))
            if w > max_w:
                max_w = w

        def can(limit: int) -> bool:
            seen = [False] * n
            seen[0] = True
            q = deque([0])
            cnt = 1
            while q:
                u = q.popleft()
                for v, w in rev[u]:
                    if w <= limit and not seen[v]:
                        seen[v] = True
                        cnt += 1
                        q.append(v)
            return cnt == n

        if not can(max_w):
            return -1

        lo, hi = 1, max_w
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if can(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        return lo
```

```
...
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxWeight(self, n: int, edges: List[List[int]], threshold: int) -> int:
        rev = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        max_w = 0

        for a, b, w in edges:
            rev[b].append((a, w))
            if w > max_w:
                max_w = w

        def can(limit: int) -> bool:
            seen = [False] * n
            seen[0] = True
            q = [0]
            head = 0
            cnt = 1

            while head < len(q):
                u = q[head]
                head += 1
                for v, w in rev[u]:
                    if w <= limit and not seen[v]:
                        seen[v] = True
                        cnt += 1
                        q.append(v)

            return cnt == n

        if not can(max_w):
            return -1

        lo, hi = 1, max_w
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if can(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid + 1

        return lo
```
